# Pre-specified analysis plan: pilot and main study

Written 2026-10-09, **before any run with the official AlphaFold3 weights**. All earlier runs used random weights and served only engineering and sizing. Any later change to this plan is logged in section 10 with its date and reason, and the paper reports it.

## 1. Questions

- **Q1 (primary).** Under identical frozen inputs, weights, seeds and AF3 settings, does the accelerator change what AF3 returns, beyond what the seed and recompilation already change? Three levels:
  1. **Decision level:** which sample is ranked first, and whether the top-ranked prediction is a success.
  2. **Structure level:** how far the same (seed, sample) moves between platforms.
  3. **Confidence level:** how much ranking score, pLDDT and ipTM move.
- **Q2.** Time, cost and device memory per structure on each platform.
- **Q3.** How many seeds are needed before conclusions (success rate, chosen structure) stop depending on the platform.

## 2. Fixed elements

**Inputs:**
- Targets: the 60 PoseBusters V2 targets in `targets/posebusters_subset.csv`, with the 10-target pilot subset marked there.
- Inputs frozen in `inputs/manifest.csv`; SHA-256 checked on every VM before running.
- MSAs from the ColabFold server, frozen. No templates.
- Ligand by CCD code. Cofactors and extra ligands are not included.

**Model:**
- AF3 v3.0.4 (commit 85c4d20), with `af3_tpu/af3_tpu.patch`.
- Official weights: `af3.bin.zst`, copied from Google's bucket on 2026-10-09. The SHA-256 is recorded per run.
- AF3 defaults: 10 recycles, 5 diffusion samples per seed. Attention `xla` in the controlled arm.

**Software stacks:**

| Platform | Stack |
|---|---|
| TPU v5e and v6e | jax/jaxlib 0.10.2 with libtpu 0.0.43.2 (validated 2026-10-09) |
| L4 and CPU | AF3's pinned stack: jax 0.10.2, CUDA 12 wheels on L4 |

**Runs:**
- One fresh process per (platform, target, seed, repetition).
- Persistent compile cache **empty** at the start of each fresh repetition.

## 3. Conditions

| Code | Platform | Attention | Role |
|---|---|---|---|
| V5 | TPU v5e (`v5litepod-1`) | xla | controlled arm |
| V6 | TPU v6e (`ct6e-standard-1t`) | xla | controlled arm |
| G | NVIDIA L4 (`g2-standard-8`) | xla | controlled arm |
| C | CPU (`n2-highmem-16`, Ice Lake) | xla | reference, on a subset |
| GT | NVIDIA L4 | triton (AF3 GPU default) | "as a user would run it" arm |

**Repetitions:**
- **r1, r2:** two fresh-cache repetitions of the same (platform, target, seed), as separate processes, to measure recompilation variability.
- **w:** one warm-cache rerun of r1 for seed 1, expected bit-identical to r1. It checks that the cache does what AF3's docs say.

## 4. Per-run outputs and metrics

For each run and each sample k = 0..4, read from AF3's output:
- the coordinates;
- `ranking_score`, `ptm`, `iptm`, mean pLDDT, `has_clash`;
- the full confidences.

**M1. Ligand success**, against the crystal structure:
- pocket = protein residues with any heavy atom within 10 Å of a reference ligand heavy atom;
- superpose the prediction on the reference using the pocket Cα atoms;
- compute the symmetry-corrected heavy-atom RMSD of the ligand;
- **Success** = RMSD < 2 Å **and** PoseBusters-valid, using the `posebusters` package's checks for docked poses (record the package version and configuration).
- A prediction that cannot be parsed or checked counts as not a success, and is reported.

**M2. Top-1 identity:**
- per seed: the sample with the highest `ranking_score` among the seed's 5;
- overall: the (seed, sample) with the highest `ranking_score` across all seeds, as AF3 ranks them.

**M3. Ranking margin:** `ranking_score` of top-1 minus top-2 within the seed. This explains flips.

**M4. Prediction-to-prediction divergence** for the same (target, seed, sample) between two conditions:
- a bit-identical flag for coordinates;
- the ligand RMSD after the same pocket superposition as M1, using the first prediction as reference;
- the protein Cα RMSD after global superposition.

**M5. Confidence divergence:** absolute differences in `ranking_score`, mean pLDDT and `iptm` for the same (target, seed, sample).

**M6. Time and cost:**
- wall, inference and compile seconds from the run records;
- cost per structure = process wall time × hourly price, at on-demand, Spot and Flex-start list prices from `cloud/prices.csv` (with dates);
- a second variant that amortises compilation over a batch of same-bucket targets.

**M7. Memory:**
- TPU and GPU: the compiled peak plus arguments, and the allocator's peak reservation;
- CPU: max RSS;
- per token bucket, plus the largest bucket that runs on each chip.

## 5. Contrasts

All contrasts are paired on (target, seed) or (target, seed, sample).

| Contrast | Pairs | Measures |
|---|---|---|
| **Recompilation** RC(P) | r1 vs r2 on platform P | variability from compiling again on the same hardware |
| **Hardware** HW(P,Q) | r1 on P vs r1 on Q | variability from the accelerator |
| **Seed** SD(P) | seed s vs seed s' on P | ordinary sampling variability, as a yardstick |
| **Default use** DU | GT vs G | effect of AF3's GPU default attention |

**Pre-specified primary hardware pairs:**
- V6 vs G: TPU against GPU;
- V5 vs V6: two TPU generations;
- G vs C: GPU against CPU.

The other pairs are reported as secondary.

**Statistics per contrast:**
- **flip rate of top-1:** fraction of pairs where the top-1 sample differs;
- **flip rate of success:** fraction of pairs where the top-1's success differs;
- **flips that matter:** the fraction where top-1 changes **and** the two chosen structures differ in success;
- median and 90th percentile of M4 and M5;
- the bit-identical fraction.

**Uncertainty:**
- 95% confidence intervals from a **paired bootstrap over targets**: resample targets with replacement, 10,000 replicates, fixed seed 2026.
- The key quantity is the difference HW(P,Q) minus RC(P) for each flip rate, with its interval.
- No p-value hunting: every pre-specified contrast is reported, whatever it shows.

**Q3 (seeds needed):** for n = 1..5 seeds, take the overall top-1 over the first n seeds and compute the success rate per platform and its spread across platforms. The answer is the smallest n where every pairwise difference in success rate has a bootstrap interval within ±2 percentage points. If none reaches that, say so.

## 6. Pilot

**Purpose:**
- check that the full pipeline works with official weights;
- measure RC and HW on a small scale;
- size the main study.

**Design:**

| Platform | Targets | Seeds | Repetitions |
|---|---|---|---|
| V5, V6, G | the 10 pilot targets | 1, 2 | r1, r2, plus w for seed 1 |
| C | the 6 pilot targets of 512 tokens or fewer | 1, 2 | r1 only |
| GT | not in the pilot | | |

**Checks before reading any result:**
1. Every run exits 0, or its failure is recorded.
2. The w rerun is bit-identical to r1 on every platform.
3. **Success-rate sanity check:** the top-1 success on V6 over the 10 pilot targets.
   - If it is below 50%, stop and investigate the inputs before the main study (ColabFold MSAs, missing cofactors, full RCSB sequence with tags).
   - Published AF3 numbers on PoseBusters are not directly comparable because our inputs differ, so this is only a sanity threshold.

**Decision**, taken when the pilot is complete (target: 20 October 2026). The pilot decides the framing and sizing of the paper, not whether it exists:

- **Outcome A, the hardware matters at decision level.** HW shows top-1 or success flips on at least one primary pair while RC on the same platform shows fewer, or the ranking margins (M3) are small enough that flips are expected at scale.
  - Main study as in section 7, with the emphasis on decision-level effects and Q3.
- **Outcome B, the hardware moves numbers but not decisions.** HW shows nonzero M4 or M5 but no decision flips, and margins are comfortable.
  - Main study still runs: a clean null at decision level is a result.
  - The paper is framed as "AF3 inference is reproducible across accelerators at the decision level, within these bounds".
  - Budget moves towards more seeds (Q3) and towards cost and memory (Q2).
- **Outcome C, the pipeline is not trustworthy:** the sanity check fails, or w is not bit-identical.
  - Fix first, then repeat the pilot.

**Caution:** with 10 targets × 2 seeds = 20 pairs per contrast, pilot flip rates have wide intervals. They size the main study; they are not reported as findings.

## 7. Main study (after the pilot)

| Platform | Targets | Seeds | Repetitions |
|---|---|---|---|
| V5, V6, G | all 60 | 1..5 | r1, r2, plus w for seed 1 on 10 targets |
| GT | all 60 | 1..5 | r1 only |
| C | the 6 pilot targets of 512 tokens or fewer | 1, 2 | r1 only, if the budget allows |

- If the pilot gives outcome B, seeds may increase beyond 5 within budget. That change goes in section 10.
- **Frozen minimal result set**, matching the venue plan: 15 November 2026.

## 8. Missing data and exclusions

- A failed run (OOM, timeout, crash) is recorded with its cause, never silently dropped.
- A (target, seed) pair enters a contrast only if both conditions have it; the paper reports the counts excluded per contrast.
- Targets are never excluded after seeing results. A target with an input problem found later is reported and analysed both with and without it.

## 9. What the paper will not claim

- Nothing from random-weight runs, apart from the engineering facts:
  - the v5e segfault and its fix;
  - the bit-identical v6e outputs across libtpu versions;
  - memory and compile figures, labelled as such.
- No "first AF3-like model on TPU": the claim is the first measured port of DeepMind's official AF3.
- No comparison with published AF3 PoseBusters success rates as if the setups were the same.
- No statement on AF3's training hardware unless checked in the AF3 Supplementary Information.

| 2026-10-12 | Correction to `analysis/collect.py`, found during the pilot2 analysis after checks 1 to 3 and before any decision. The implementation took `ranking_score` from `*_summary_confidences.json`, which AlphaFold3 writes rounded to 2 decimals. The top score was then tied in most (target, seed) units, ties went to the lowest sample index, and the analysis's top-1 differed from AlphaFold3's in about half of the units. M2 (section 4) is the sample with the highest `ranking_score` as AlphaFold3 ranks them, so the full-precision value from `<target>_ranking_scores.csv` (written by AlphaFold3 in the same output folder) is used for M2, M3, the flip rates, HW minus RC, Q3, check 3 and the M5 ranking-score difference; the rounded value is kept in a separate column. Independent check on all 252 pilot2 (run, target) outputs: the highest full-precision score has no ties and is always the sample AlphaFold3 itself writes as its top-level model (mmCIF identical apart from the timestamp line); the tie rule (lowest sample index) is kept but no longer used. ipTM is written only at 2 decimals by AlphaFold3 and is used as written. Check 3: 50% with the rounded value, 60% with the full value; both pass. Checks 1 and 2, M1, M4, M6 and M7 are unaffected. The outputs of the defective implementation are kept unchanged in the analysis folder and are not used. In the same change, the report's `primary` column is renamed so that it cannot be read as a section 5 primary pair. | Implementation defect; the plan's definition is unchanged |
| 2026-10-12 | Pilot decision: **Outcome A** (section 6), taken by applying the pre-specified rule to the pilot2 decision inputs, after the corrected analysis and before any main-study run. Outcome C does not apply: checks 1 to 3 pass on every arm (check 2 as 2a, 2b and strict bit-identity on V5, V6, G and Gd) and the M2 cross-check holds on every output. Outcome B does not apply: top-1 flips occur on primary hardware pairs. Outcome A applies on both of its criteria: (1) at least one primary pair (V5 against V6, and V6 against G) shows top-1 flips, and V5 against V6 also success flips, while RC on the same platforms shows none; (2) the M3 ranking margins are of the same order as the ranking-score differences between platforms, so flips are expected at scale. As section 6 states, these pilot rates have wide intervals and are not findings: success flips on the primary pairs are rare and their intervals include zero, and RC(G) itself shows flips (compile-time autotuning), which the Gd arm separates. The decision inputs are in `results/analysis/pilot2_20261012/step2/` (gitignored; regenerated by `reproduce_step2.sh` at commit `d804922`) and will be reported in the supplementary material as pilot figures. Consequence: the main study runs as in section 7, with the emphasis on decision-level effects and Q3. Any change to its design (arms, seeds, provisioning) is recorded in its own row before the main runs. | Section 6 decision, recorded before the main study |
| 2026-10-12 | Main study design after Outcome A, before any main-study run. (a) As section 7: V5, V6 and G on all 60 targets, seeds 1 to 5, r1 and r2, plus w for seed 1 on the 10 pilot targets; GT (`l4_triton`) on all 60 targets, seeds 1 to 5, r1 only; C on the 6 pilot targets of 512 tokens or fewer, seeds 1 and 2, r1 only (the budget allows it). Seeds are not increased beyond 5: that option was for Outcome B. (b) As section 11, at its minimum: V6h and Gh on all 60 targets, seeds 1 and 2, r1 only; they are not extended. (c) Added: Gd (`l4_xla_det`) with the same design as G (60 targets, seeds 1 to 5, r1 and r2, w for seed 1 on the 10 pilot targets), entering only the contrasts and roles fixed in the 2026-10-11 rows (RC(Gd), W(Gd), DET, HW(V6,Gd), HW(Gd,C), all secondary). Reason: in pilot2, RC(G) shows compile-time variability from autotuning, which Gd removes, so Gd separates the platform effect from autotuning in the V6 against L4 comparison. The section 5 primary pairs and every other pre-specified role are unchanged. | Section 6 consequence of Outcome A; Gd decided with the pilot |

(a) Provisioning: V5, G, GT, Gd, Gh and C on demand; V6 and V6h on demand (`PROVISIONING=standard`), as the pilot2 V6 arm, because Flex-start had no v6e capacity from 2026-10-10 to 2026-10-12. The provisioning model is recorded per session and enters no contrast; M6 is unchanged. (b) Parts: the runs are split into plan parts (`harness/plans/main/`), sized from the pilot2 run records so that each part fits one VM's lifetime. All runs of one (arm, target) are in the same part, so r1, r2 and w of a target run on one VM, and w reads the cache its own r1 wrote on that VM. VMs of the same machine type are treated as the same platform; the session (VM) is recorded for every run. Parts are a scheduling device, not a factor. (c) A part that fails or is interrupted is relaunched unchanged and every launch is recorded here; no run is dropped or selected on results (section 8). | How the main study is run, fixed before it starts |

| 2026-10-13 | Cross-session check, specified before any main-study outcome was read and before any pilot2 output was compared with a main-study output. Each setup builds AlphaFold3 from source, and builds differ in build-metadata bytes (temporary paths, build times, linker identifiers); within a platform, RC and W compare runs on the same VM, but PR (V6 against V6h, G against Gh) and DET (G against Gd) compare runs on different VMs of the same platform. To test whether VM-to-VM build differences reach the outputs, after the main runs finish, `harness/compare_runs.py --strict` (only the mmCIF timestamp line masked) compares, for the 10 pilot targets, each pilot2 r1 run with the main-study r1 run of the same condition, target and seed: seeds 1 and 2 for V5, V6, G, Gd, V6h and Gh, and C on its 6 targets (G added on 2026-10-13, before any comparison was made, to match the expectation sentence below). The AlphaFold3 code, patch, harness run code, configs (apart from one description string), frozen inputs, weights and TPU stack are the same in both (commits `2a0122c`, `6021024` and `f3fdff6` against `501637f`). Expected: bit-identical on V5, V6, V6h, Gd and C (deterministic compiles, as RC shows in pilot2); G and Gh are reported descriptively, since their default autotuning varies between compiles (RC(G) in pilot2). This is an engineering check: it reports identical or not per (condition, target, seed) and enters no contrast; a mismatch where identity is expected is reported and investigated, and no run is dropped. | Show whether build differences between VMs can affect the outputs that PR and DET compare |
| 2026-10-13 | Training-cutoff overlap, recorded before any main-study outcome was read. The released AlphaFold3 weights were trained on structures up to 2021-09-30. From the RCSB initial release dates in the frozen target records, 21 of the 60 targets were released on or before that date (7D5C, the earliest, on 2021-02-24; 7NF3, the latest, on 2021-08-04; 6 in bucket 256, 11 in 512, 2 in 768, 2 in 1024; 4 of them pilot targets: 7D5C, 7BTT, 7NPL, 7NP6), so they may have been in AlphaFold3's training data; the target selection rule did not use release dates. No self-template can occur: the inputs carry no templates and no template search runs (section 2), and `--max_template_date` keeps AlphaFold3's default 2021-09-30, which only affects a CCD coordinate fallback. The overlap does not bias the paired contrasts, since every condition sees the same targets, but it may raise absolute success rates. Added sensitivity analysis, secondary: M1 success rate per condition, Q3, and the top-1 and success flip rates of the section 5 primary pairs (with HW minus RC) are also reported on the 39 targets released after 2021-09-30, next to the full-set values; the primary analysis is unchanged. | Absolute success rates are open to a training-overlap objection |

| 2026-10-13 and 2026-10-14 | Chemical Component Dictionary (CCD) at build time. Every setup builds AlphaFold3 from source, and that build (libcifpp) downloads `components.cif` from files.wwpdb.org; `build_data` then turns it into the CCD pickle AlphaFold3 uses for every residue and ligand given by CCD code. The file is not among the frozen inputs (section 2), and the sessions do not record which version they received: the 63 session setups with a `build_data` step log only the file's path (no date, size or checksum), and the file is not uploaded. Check, made before any main-study outcome was read: the frozen inputs use 80 CCD codes, the 60 study ligands (one per target, all distinct) and the 20 standard amino acids; they contain no modified residue, ion, nucleic acid, unknown residue or user-defined CCD. A copy downloaded on 2026-10-08 (10:27 UTC, local AlphaFold3 build) and six copies downloaded on 2026-10-13 (local builds, 11:30 to 11:45 UTC) are byte-identical whole files (519,231,791 bytes, SHA-256 `f1bef735f3d7...`), so all 80 entries, and everything AlphaFold3 derives from the whole file, were the same at both ends of the window; per entry, the 80 data blocks are identical, and none of the 80 was modified after 2026-04-24 (the latest date in the whole file is a modification of 2026-10-02). The file wwPDB served on 2026-10-13 (12:20 UTC) is this same file and carries `Last-Modified: 2026-10-03 08:22 UTC`, so it was not replaced between 2026-10-03 and 2026-10-13: every session set up in that window, which includes every pilot2 and main-study session so far, downloaded this file, although the sessions themselves do not record it. Setups after a later wwPDB update may receive a newer file. Recheck on 2026-10-14 at 06:07 UTC (`ccd_recheck.sh`, after every main-study run had finished): wwPDB now serves a newer file (519,775,453 bytes, SHA-256 `3db18637ad7f...`, `Last-Modified: 2026-10-10 05:56 UTC`; latest dates in the file: initial release 2026-10-07, modification 2026-10-09), and in it the 80 data blocks of the frozen inputs' CCD codes are identical to the 2026-10-08 copy. The newer file was not yet served at 12:20 UTC on 2026-10-13, so it was published between then and 06:07 UTC on 2026-10-14, most likely at the wwPDB weekly release (Wednesday 2026-10-14, 00:00 UTC). The last two main-study sessions started their jobs, whose setup downloads the file, at 20:42 and 20:56 UTC on 2026-10-13 (`main_gd_08` and `main_v6_01`), before that release; under that reading every pilot2 and main-study session used the `f1bef735...` file. Either way, the 80 entries the frozen inputs use are identical in both files. Tables: `results/analysis/main_monitor_20261013/ccd_codes.tsv` and `ccd_codes_recheck_20261014.tsv`. | An AlphaFold3 input that the frozen inputs do not cover |
| 2026-10-13 | AlphaFold3's compiled extension is not byte-reproducible across builds. Each setup builds AlphaFold3 from source in a new temporary build folder (scikit-build-core, through `uv sync`), and the compiled extension (`alphafold3/cpp`) embeds that folder's path (source paths of the C++ dependencies), the build time (dssp and libcifpp version strings and one compiled constant) and libcifpp's temporary install-staging path; the linker's binary identifier and signature, and static-archive member times, follow from them. Evidence (local builds on macOS arm64; `evidence_macos.txt`): two source builds of AlphaFold3 v3.0.4 with `af3_tpu.patch`, minutes apart, have 154 of 157 installed AlphaFold3 files byte-identical (every Python module and data file, the CCD included) and differ in the extension, `lib/libcifpp.a` and `lib/cmake/cifpp/cifpp-targets.cmake`, in those build-metadata bytes; with a fixed build folder and `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH`, these three are identical once only the staging path, the linker identifiers and the archive member times are masked. Consequence: within a platform, the extension's bytes differ between sessions (VMs) in build metadata; the differing bytes encode build paths and build times only, and how the code uses them was not traced. Limits: the evidence comes from macOS (Apple clang, Mach-O), not from the Linux VMs (GCC, ELF), whose binaries were not kept; the build mechanism is the same there, but no VM bytes were compared. Whether these differences reach the outputs is tested by the cross-session check of 2026-10-13 (section 10), after the main runs finish. Every main-study part keeps this setup unchanged; the prebuilt-wheel alternative is not applied during the main study (decision of 2026-10-13). | Record a property of the software as run, found before any main-study outcome was read |
| 2026-10-14 | Main study complete; no run was dropped or selected on results. Each of the 53 parts has exactly one session with a job, and in each the job and the launcher exited 0; no part has a second such session, so the duplicate-session rule (row of 2026-10-13) has nothing to choose. The 53 sessions hold 2,992 target runs, as the design gives (V5, V6, G and Gd 610 each; GT 300; V6h and Gh 120 each; C 12), counted from the per-target ranking files without reading them. Commits: 38 sessions were launched from `501637f`, 13 from `0dec27a` and 2 from `e529fb0`, all with 0 uncommitted files; between these commits only `notes/analysis_plan.md` and `analysis/` changed, and neither is uploaded to the VMs. The git blob hashes of the uploaded files are the same in all 53 sessions, except that 43 also carried `harness/.DS_Store`, a macOS Finder file that git ignores and no script reads. While the runs were in progress, checks 1, 2 (2a, 2b and `compare_runs --strict`) and provenance passed on the parts finished by 11:51 UTC (28) and by 17:55 UTC (43) on 2026-10-13. The checks on all 53 parts, the pre-specified analysis and the cross-session check follow this row; no main-study outcome had been read when it was written. | Sections 7 and 8 |
| 2026-10-14 | M1 for three glycan ligands, found in the main-study analysis after the primary results were computed, and recorded before the analyses (2) and (3) below were run. For 7OZC (G6S), 7WDT (NGS) and 7WJB (BGC), every predicted ligand lacks O1, in every condition (735 samples). At its default `--fix_standalone_glycans=false`, AlphaFold3 v3.0.4 deletes the atom named O1 from every ligand in its glycan lists (`GLYCAN_LINKING_LIGANDS`, `GLYCAN_OTHER_LIGANDS`) unless O1 is bonded through `bondedAtomPairs` (`model/pipeline/structure_cleaning.py`, `atom_layout.get_link_drop_atoms`); its help text states that AlphaFold3 training and evaluation used this regime. For these three ligands O1 is the anomeric hydroxyl, flagged as a leaving atom in the CCD. The two other study ligands in AlphaFold3's glycan lists (7BCP GCO, 7DKT GLF) have no atom named O1 and are scored normally. `analysis/score_ligand.py` requires every reference heavy atom, so M1 and M4's ligand RMSD are not computed for these samples. Decision: (1) Primary analysis: section 4 as written; these samples count as not a success, in every condition, and are reported. (2) Section 8 (a target with an input problem found later is analysed both with and without it): every result that uses M1 (success rates, check 3, Q3, success flips, flips that matter, M4's ligand RMSD, the training-cutoff sensitivity) is also reported on the other 57 targets. (3) Post-hoc sensitivity, labelled as such: the three targets are scored on the heavy atoms AlphaFold3 predicts, that is the reference ligand without O1, with the same pocket, superposition, symmetry correction and PoseBusters `dock` checks; only atoms removed by this rule are dropped from the reference. (1), (2) and (3) are reported together. Top-1 flips, M2, M3, M5, M6 and M7 do not use M1 and are unchanged. The flag stays at its default and no run is repeated. (4) Descriptive, outside every contrast: how the rule acts on the full PoseBusters V1 and V2 sets (CCD entries of their ligands against AlphaFold3's glycan lists), checked on AlphaFold3's own input processing with a local test that uses no weights. | M1 cannot be computed as specified for three targets |
| 2026-10-14 | Correction to the implementation of M4, found while running analysis (3) of the glycan row, after the primary results were computed. Section 4 defines M4's C-alpha RMSD on the protein after global superposition; it does not use the ligand or M1. The implementation computed it in the same call as the ligand scoring, which stopped at the missing O1, so the primary tables have no C-alpha RMSD for the 1,365 sample pairs of 7OZC, 7WDT and 7WJB. Correction: the C-alpha RMSD is computed for every sample pair, independently of the ligand scoring; for the other 57 targets the values are unchanged (identical in every pair), and for the three targets they are the values computed in analysis (3), whose protein superposition does not depend on the ligand. Primary M4 C-alpha medians are reported over all pairs (for example HW(V5,V6) 0.0445 Å and HW(V6,G) 0.0766 Å, against 0.0421 Å and 0.0705 Å without the three targets). No other result changes. In analysis (3) the pocket is the one defined by the full reference ligand, O1 included, as section 4 defines it. | Implementation error; the plan's definition is unchanged |
| 2026-10-14 | Memory limit per chip (M7, "the largest bucket that runs on each chip"), specified after the main study and before any run of it. All 60 targets are at most 1,024 tokens and every bucket ran on every platform, so the main study does not reach M7's largest bucket. Measurement, descriptive and outside every contrast (label `memlimit`): the largest frozen input, 7D5C (1,023 tokens), official weights, seed 1, AlphaFold3 defaults otherwise, one fresh process per bucket size, padded to a single bucket with AlphaFold3's own `--buckets` flag, in AlphaFold3's default bucket sequence above 1,024: 1,280, 1,536, 2,048, 2,560, 3,072, 3,584, 4,096, 4,608 and 5,120 tokens. Padding gives the model the array shapes of a real input of that bucket (same MSA rows, no templates); it is not a real input of that size, and the paper says so. Platforms and settings as in the main study: V5, V6 (on demand), G and GT (L4, on demand), no unified memory. Stop rule, per platform: sizes run in increasing order and stop at the first size that fails (out of memory or any other error, recorded with its cause) or whose process exceeds 4 hours; that size and the last completed size are reported. Recorded per process: exit status and cause, compiled peak plus arguments and the allocator peak (M7), compile, inference and wall time, and cost per structure (M6). The predicted structures are not read for any finding. | Q2's largest bucket is not reached by the main study's targets |
| 2026-10-14 | Exploratory analysis of why top-1 flips occur, specified after the main-study outcomes were read and before any of it was computed; labelled exploratory in the paper, and no primary result changes. On the main-study r1 data, for HW(V5,V6), HW(V6,G), HW(V6,GT), RC(G), DU, DET, PR(V6), PR(G) and HWh(V6,G): (a) per-seed top-1 flip rate against the M3 margin of the first-named condition, in margin quintiles and as a logistic regression of flip on log margin; (b) concentration over targets: the distribution of the number of seeds with a flip per target, and the share of all flips from the 10% of targets with most flips; (c) perturbation size: the median absolute ranking-score difference of the same (seed, sample) between the two conditions (M5), against the median M3 margin, per contrast, and the flip rate predicted from these two quantities against the observed one; (d) flips by token bucket; (e) for flips that matter, the ligand RMSD to the crystal of the two top-1 samples. Intervals are target-clustered bootstrap, as in section 5. | Explain the primary result; no new run |

| 2026-10-14 | Follow-up experiment 1, protein-protein complexes (label `ppi`), specified after the main-study outcomes were read and before any target was selected or any run made; reported as a follow-up, outside the pre-registered study, whatever it shows. Question: do the main study's decision-level effects (Q1) also occur for protein-protein complexes? Targets, by a fixed rule: RCSB entries released 2021-10-01 to 2024-12-31 (after the weights' training cutoff), X-ray at 2.5 Å or better, exactly two protein entities with one chain each in the first biological assembly and no nucleic acid, each chain 30 to 700 residues, at most 1,000 residues in total, and the two chains in contact (at least 10 residue pairs with heavy atoms within 5 Å); redundancy removed by 30% sequence identity (an entry is dropped if either chain is at least 30% identical to a chain of an entry already kept, entries taken in random order with seed 2026); then 30 entries drawn at random with seed 2026 from the remaining pool (all of them if fewer than 30). Inputs: the two protein chains only (no ligands, ions or waters), unpaired MSAs from the ColabFold server in the main study's mode plus a paired MSA from its pairing endpoint, frozen with SHA-256 like the main inputs; no templates. Å target whose input cannot be built is replaced by the next draw, before any run. Conditions as in the main study: V5, V6 and G, seeds 1 to 5, r1; r2 on V6 and G. Contrasts: HW(V5,V6) and HW(V6,G), RC(V6) and RC(G), HW minus RC(P), each with the main study's statistics and target-clustered bootstrap. Metrics: top-1 identity per seed and overall (M2), M3, M4 (C-alpha RMSD after global superposition), M5; success of a prediction is DockQ of at least 0.23 against the deposited structure (DockQ 2, recorded version), with DockQ of at least 0.49 reported as secondary. | 2026-10-18 | Follow-up experiment 5, host-side featurisation (label `feat`), specified after the main-study and follow-up outcomes were read and before any run; reported as a follow-up whatever it shows. Question: are AlphaFold3's input features identical across the host CPUs of the arms? AlphaFold3 featurises each (target, seed) on the host CPU before the model runs (`data/featurisation.py`), including an RDKit reference conformer per ligand from a seed derived from the model seed (`model/features.py`, `get_random_conformer`, ETKDG), a floating-point computation outside the accelerator. The arms ran on different host CPUs (session records): v5e AMD EPYC 7B13, v6e AMD EPYC 9B14, L4 and A100 Intel Xeon at 2.20 GHz, C Intel Xeon at 2.60 GHz. Within one host family the features are already known to be stable (RC 0 on TPU, cross-session identity, bit-identical G r1 and r2 trunk embeddings in follow-up 2); across families they were never compared, so the attribution of the HW contrasts to the accelerator rather than to the host rests on it. Design: AlphaFold3's own featurisation only (no model, no accelerator; AlphaFold3 code unchanged, called from a harness script), for the 60 PoseBusters targets and the 30 follow-up 1 targets, seeds 1 to 5, with the frozen inputs and the same AlphaFold3 build, lockfile and CCD as the arms; run once on a host of each of the four CPU families, matching each arm's CPU model and, as far as the cloud allows, its OS image (details fixed in an implementation row before any run). Measure: SHA-256 of every feature array per (target, seed), and the list of arrays that differ. Decision rule, fixed now: if every array is identical on every host for every (target, seed), the host is excluded as a source of the HW differences and the paper says so. If any array differs, the differing (target, seed, array) are reported, and follow-up 5b is run: features computed once on a single host are given unchanged to V5, V6 and G for the 10 pilot targets, seeds 1 and 2, r1, and the per-seed top-1 flips of HW(V5,V6) and HW(V6,G) are reported next to the main-study values; its implementation is recorded before any 5b run. It runs on a local machine, `naspaz8` (Ubuntu 24.04 VM, 12 vCPU, 104 GiB, `--jackhmmer_n_cpu=3`), one target at a time; before this row the pipeline had run only on AlphaFold3's README example 2PV7, which is not a study target. Outputs: one `<target>_data.json` per target (MSAs and up to 4 templates), with log, wall time and peak memory, frozen with a SHA-256 manifest before any inference run. Determinism check: the 10 pilot targets run a second time on `naspaz8`, and once on a second local machine, `naspaz6` (Intel Core Ultra 7 255H, `--jackhmmer_n_cpu=4`, the same image and databases, their SHA-256 checked there); the `_data.json` SHA-256 are compared, and any difference is reported by field (MSA rows, template hits); the first `naspaz8` run is used for inference whatever the check shows. Descriptive analyses, without inference: per target, official against ColabFold, the number of MSA rows and Neff (sequence weights at 80% identity; implementation fixed before the analysis); the template audit, giving for each template its PDB ID, release date, sequence identity to the query and whether that entry contains the target's ligand (CCD code), with the target's own entry and templates at 95% identity or more flagged; pipeline wall time and peak memory per target. Inference arms, with the settings of V5, V6 and G and only the input changed (the `_data.json`, one seed per run as in the main study), on demand: Go (the settings of G; r1 and r2), V6o (r1), V5o (r1), plus w for seed 1 on the 10 pilot targets for V5o and V6o; 60 targets, seeds 1 to 5. Contrasts, with the statistics of section 5 and the target-clustered bootstrap (10,000 replicates, seed 2026): HW(V5o,V6o) and HW(V6o,Go), the two section 5 primary pairs that do not involve C, with official inputs; HW(V5o,Go); RC(Go); HW(V6o,Go) minus RC(Go); W(V5o), W(V6o); SD for each condition. RC(V5o) and RC(V6o) are not run (cost): the main study's RC(V5) and RC(V6) are cited, not assumed for the new inputs. Comparison with the main study, on the same (target, seed): for HW(V5,V6), HW(V6,G) and HW(V5,G), each flip rate official minus ColabFold, with a paired bootstrap interval; the input contrast IN(P), P against Po on the same chip (ColabFold against official inputs), for P in V5, V6 and G, as a yardstick; M1 success rate per condition. Reading rule, fixed now: the main conclusion holds with official inputs if HW(V6o,Go) minus RC(Go) and HW(V5o,V6o) both have a 95% interval above 0; for each pair, the official-minus-ColabFold difference is read as lower, similar or higher if its interval lies entirely below 0, contains 0 or lies entirely above 0. The official inputs change MSAs and templates together, so no difference is attributed to either one alone. Every result that uses M1 is also reported on the 39 targets released after 2021-09-30 (row of 2026-10-13), since the templates of the 21 earlier targets may include their own or near-identical entries. Stop rules: if the data pipeline has not finished all 60 targets by 2026-10-28, no inference arm runs and the follow-up is reported as descriptive only; inference runs only if launched by 2026-11-02, and arms not finished by 2026-11-08 are reported as incomplete. Launch order: Go r1, V6o, V5o, Go r2, each with its w runs. Budget: an arm is launched only if the credit balance read before its launch, minus its estimated cost, stays at or above 100 USD; cost limit 140 USD for the inference arms. (g) Analysis (`analysis/msa.py`, `scripts/analysis_runs/msa/reproduce.sh`; the descriptive part runs after the ingest, the rest after the arms): choices the row leaves open, by their most literal reading: Neff is the sum of row weights, a row's weight 1 over the rows (itself included) at identity 0.8 or more, identity over all columns of the a3m with insertions removed and a gap matching a gap (Morcos et al. 2011); the template audit reads PDB ID, chain, release date and sequence from each template's mmCIF and the entry's ligands (and a missing release date) from RCSB, cached; template identity is over the aligned pairs; the reading rule names no statistic: its headline is the top-1 flip rate, the statistic the main study's primary result is about (section 5 lists three flip rates without ranking them, `notes/analysis_plan.md` lines 108 to 110 and 116; the row of 2026-10-14 that explains "the primary result" analyses the per-seed top-1 flip rate, line 224; the follow-up 5b design reports per-seed top-1 flips, line 240; `analysis/contrasts.py` line 105 and the primary_v2 report list it first), and `analysis/msa.py` writes that branch first; the success flip rate and flips that matter are reported alongside as secondary; "every result that uses M1" is the success flip, flips that matter, success rates and the rule and comparisons on them; IN(P) pairs the main study's P r1 with Po r1 on the same (target, seed); an arm with fewer (target, seed) than planned is reported as incomplete and every result that uses it says so, nothing is imputed. Go r1 and r2 run on separate VMs, as the launch order requires, while the main study's G r1 and r2 ran in the same VM: RC(Go) spans two VMs, unlike the main study's RC(G), so it may be larger, which makes the test of HW(V6o,Go) minus RC(Go) conservative. If Go r2 is not launched (the 140 USD cap on the running spend; Go r2 is last), RC(Go) is unavailable: HW(V6o,Go) minus RC(Go) is reported as not available, the reading rule is not applied to that pair, and the main study's RC(G) is not substituted. V6o is on demand, as V6 was (`launch_record.txt` of the three main-study V6 sessions: `PROVISIONING=standard`; `cloud/af3_run_many.sh` gives every v6e part `PROVISIONING=standard`, and `cloud/af3_run.sh` refuses Spot on v6e). The featurisation allowance (x 2) is an assumption: `scripts/monitor/msa_feat_check.py` compares each fetched part's featurisation seconds with its plan's allowance (2 x the main study's median for the same arm and bucket) and its job time with the expected one, prints a warning for a part that exceeds either, and changes no plan; it is run after the first Go r1 part, and any change of plan is made by hand with a section 10 row. The smoke tests run on 2PV7 only. | 2026-10-29 | Follow-up experiment 7, the software stack on the same chip (label `sw`), specified after the main-study and follow-up 1 to 5 outcomes were read and before any run with another stack; reported as a follow-up, outside the pre-registered study, whatever it shows (drafted on 2026-10-27 in branch `fu7-10`, commits `e514f55`, `27018b1` and `7f95695`, while follow-up 6 ran; merged into `main` in `e74104a` after the last follow-up 6 arm; entered here unchanged before any run of follow-ups 7 to 9). Question: how much do the top-1 and the success change when only the JAX/XLA stack changes on the same chip, compared with how much they change between chips? Feasibility, checked on 2026-10-27 on the Mac (CPU, random weights, 6YQW, one seed; no cloud resource): AlphaFold3 v3.0.4 (`85c4d205`) unchanged needs jax 0.10.x. With jax and jaxlib 0.10.1 and every other pin of AlphaFold3's `uv.lock` unchanged (flax 0.12.2, dm-haiku 0.0.16, tokamax 0.0.12, qwix 0.1.5) it imports, compiles and runs end to end (exit 0; the structure differs from the jax 0.10.2 one, largest coordinate difference 2.49 A, same ranking score). jax 0.11.0 fails at import (flax 0.12.2 uses `jax.core.Effect`, removed in 0.11.0), and jax 0.11.2 with the newest flax (0.12.12) fails at import in dm-haiku 0.0.16 (`jax.core.DropVar`, removed); a 0.11 stack would change flax and dm-haiku as well, not the XLA stack alone, and is out of scope. Alternative stack (the previous jax release; jaxlib bundles XLA): jax and jaxlib 0.10.1 with libtpu 0.0.41 on TPU (the libtpu that `jax[tpu]==0.10.1` pins) and with jax-cuda12-plugin and jax-cuda12-pjrt 0.10.1 on GPU. AlphaFold3 code, `af3_tpu.patch`, weights (private bucket, as in the main study), configs, environment, image, machine type and inputs unchanged. Arms: V6s, the settings of V6 with the alternative stack, and Gs, the settings of G with it; 60 targets, seeds 1 to 5, r1, one seed per process. Contrasts, paired on (target, seed) and (target, seed, sample) with the statistics of section 5 and the target-clustered bootstrap (10,000 replicates, seed 2026): SW(V6) = V6 r1 against V6s r1 and SW(G) = G r1 against Gs r1, the main study's V6 and G r1 as the reference side. Comparison, on the (target, seed) both have, resampling targets: each flip rate of SW(P) minus that of RC(G) and minus that of HW(V6,G) (main-study pairs), read as lower, similar or higher if the 95% interval lies below 0, contains 0 or lies above 0. Reading rule, fixed now: headline the top-1 flip rate; the software stack "moves the top-1 less than the chip" if SW(P) minus HW(V6,G) lies below 0, and "as much as the chip" otherwise; the success flip rate and flips that matter are secondary, and every result that uses M1 is also given on the 39 targets released after 2021-09-30. Provisioning, a change in how the VMs are obtained, not in the computation (machine type, image, software and settings are those of the source arm; each session records its provisioning): the main study ran on demand on every platform (`PROVISIONING=standard` in all 53 main-study launch records); V6s runs on demand, as V6 did (v6e has had no Flex-start capacity), and Gs runs on Spot. A preempted part is relaunched unchanged (`ALLOW_RELAUNCH` with its plan name), and the relaunch is recorded in section 10; no run is dropped or selected on results. A smoke test (label `sw_smoke`, an engineering check, never a finding) runs 2PV7 with each arm's settings, stack and provisioning before the arm; if the alternative stack fails there on a platform (for example a compile failure of libtpu 0.0.41 on v6e, as 0.0.42.1 failed on v5e), that arm is not run and the limitation is reported. Stop rules: launch by 2026-11-11; an arm not finished by 2026-11-12 is reported as incomplete. | The main study measures the chip with the software fixed; a reader needs the size of an ordinary software change as a yardstick |

Both arms are launched from the one launch host, the Mac, from the main checkout. Each arm, and from now on every arm, is launched with `~/Desktop/af3_drafts/retry_arm_par.sh`, which reads the summary of its own wrapper run instead of the newest one, and with `ALLOW_OTHER_LAUNCHERS=1`. Without that setting `cloud/af3_run_many.sh` refuses to start while any `af3_run.sh` of another arm runs; a round of either wrapper would then do nothing and wait 20 min, up to 12 times. GPU launchers in total stay within the GPUS_ALL_REGIONS quota of 8: while H or H r2 runs (3 H100 at a time, `MAX_H100=3`), an L4 arm runs with `MAX_L4=5`; otherwise `MAX_L4=8`. TPU arms run one part at a time (`MAX_V5E=1`, `MAX_V6E=1`, the defaults). The budget rule of each follow-up subtracts the expected cost of every arm still running (`scripts/monitor/credit_check_fu11_12.sh`, in branch `fu11-12`, merged into `main` with the follow-up 11 implementation). Order within each family: TPU V5r, V6r, then V6h5; GPU Gr, then Gh5. Follow-up 12 starts after every follow-up 11 arm has finished, as its row says. H r2 is launched with `retry_arm_par.sh` too, so a follow-up 11 GPU arm can run beside it. Checked for shared state between two concurrent wrappers (scripts read, 2026-11-03): run folders `results/af3/.many/<UTC second>/` (distinct unless two wrappers start a round in the same second; the second then stops with "exists" and retries 20 min later); part locks `results/af3/.many/locks/<platform>_<plan>` (plan names distinct per arm); session and VM names (UTC second plus platform and plan; distinct across platforms); temporary files (`mktemp`); quota counters (per wrapper, hence the platform rule and `MAX_L4`). | 2026-11-04 | Follow-up experiment 10, larger inputs on the whole PDB (label `large`), specified after the main-study and follow-up outcomes were read and before any input of its targets was built or any run was made; reported as a follow-up, outside the pre-registered study, whatever it shows. It replaces the deferred design of 2026-10-29 and follows the Runs N' Poses screen of 2026-11-04 (11 independent large targets, not run). Question: do hardware-induced top-1 flips beyond recompilation grow or shrink when the input grows from at most 1,024 tokens to 1,025 to 1,536? (a) Targets: `targets/large/pdb_screen.py --ligand_rule amended`, read-only on the RCSB PDB (search of 2026-11-04T12:46:26Z; every answer cached under `data/pdb_large_screen/`, so the screen reruns offline with identical outputs). Source: entries released after 2021-09-30, X-ray or EM, resolution at most 3.0 Å, one protein entity, no nucleic acid, at least one non-polymer entity. Rule, fixed before any prediction: 1. one polypeptide(L) entity whose RCSB canonical sequence has the 20 standard letters only; not one of the main study's 60 or follow-up 11's 72 entries. 2. one ligand: the candidates are the entry's non-polymer components of 150 to 1,000 Da in AlphaFold 3's CCD that are not on the screen's additive, ion and cofactor list and not excluded by the amended rule (CCD type other than non-polymer; a PSI-MOD annotation; no carbon, or an element other than C, H, N, O, S, P, F, Cl, Br, I, B, Se, Si; an acyclic chain of 8 or more carbons; a nucleoside 5', 2' or 3' phosphate; three consecutive ethylene-glycol units; the named list `EXTRA` of buffers, chelators, a detergent, polyamines, cofactors, chromophores and ergosterol). Of all their instances in model 1 of the deposited mmCIF that RCSB does not annotate as covalently linked, the one with the most protein heavy atoms within 4 Å of its heavy atoms; ties, the lowest `label_asym_id`. An entry whose instance touches no protein atom is excluded. 3. Single-chain pocket: every protein heavy atom within 6 Å of that instance's heavy atoms (all alternate locations counted) is in one chain. 4. Size: AlphaFold 3's token count of that chain plus one copy of the ligand by CCD code (empty MSA, no templates; `inputs/af3_featurise.py`): large 1,025 to 1,536; control at most 1,024; 1,537 to 2,048 recorded for information, not run. 5. One target per RCSB 30% sequence cluster (Search API grouping; an entity not yet clustered is its own cluster): the member that meets rules 1 to 4 with the best resolution, then the earliest release, then the lowest PDB ID. 6. Control: clusters not in the large group, in strata of method (X-ray, EM) and resolution band (at most 2.0, 2.0 to 2.5, 2.5 to 3.0 Å), as many as the large group has in each; `numpy.random.default_rng(2026)` permutes each stratum's cluster IDs sorted as strings (strata in a fixed order); each cluster in turn gives its first member in that stratum that meets rules 1 to 4, until the stratum is full. 7. Variant A: the targets with exactly one deposited polymer chain. Counts in the large band (950 to 2,048 residues, every entry checked): 723 entries from the search (158 clusters); after rule 1, 712; rule 2, 204 (53 clusters); rule 3, 168 (46 clusters; it removes 36 entries and 7 clusters); 1,025 to 1,536 tokens, 128 entries in 34 clusters. Selected: large 34 (X-ray 12, EM 22; bucket 1,280: 24, 1,536: 10; variant A 17); control 34 (the same strata; buckets 256: 3, 512: 20, 768: 7, 1,024: 4; variant A 11; 62 entries checked in draw order); 1,537 to 2,048, 10 (not run). Every target was released after the training cutoff. List: `targets/large/pdb_targets_amended.csv`. Under the screen's lists alone (the literal rule), lipids, nucleotide cofactors, buffers, a modified residue and covalent ligands pass rule 2; that selection (60 large) is kept in `targets/large/pdb_targets.csv` for the record and not used. The amended ligand rule was fixed in four passes, each made after reading the candidate lists of the previous pass (ligand identities, no prediction: none exists for these targets) because they still held components the rule meant to exclude; the large group was the same over the last three passes, and every pass redrew the control group from the same seed. Code: `targets/large/pdb_screen.py` in branch `fu10`, merged into `main` with the follow-up 10 implementation. (b) Inputs: as the main study's: unpaired ColabFold MSAs from the public server (mode `env`), no templates, the ligand by CCD code, seeds 1 to 5, frozen with SHA-256 before any run. A target is replaced only for a permanent cause (AlphaFold 3 rejects its input, or its token count differs from the screen's): by the next member of its cluster that meets rules 1 to 4 (large) or the next cluster of its stratum's permutation (control), recorded in a row before any run. A server refusal or timeout is not a cause; the build stops and reruns from the cache. (c) Arms, main-study settings unchanged: V5 (`tpu_xla`, v5e, on demand), V6 (`tpu_xla`, v6e, Flex-start), G (`l4_xla`, L4, Spot). r1 on all 68 targets. r2 on G for all 68. r2 on V5 and V6 for a fixed subset of 10 large and 10 control targets, drawn by `numpy.random.default_rng(2026).choice` without replacement from each group's PDB IDs sorted as strings, large first: 7DIA, 7QUR, 8C8V, 8SX7, 8TZG, 8V1G, 8XB3, 9BWT, 9GZ2, 9X5O (large); 7UMP, 8BLP, 8CEY, 9ETQ, 9HKX, 9OHN, 9OMM, 9P4Y, 9ZB9, 9ZNO (control). TPU recompilation was bit-identical on all 60 main-study targets; if any subset target's r2 on V5 or V6 is not bit-identical to its r1, r2 on that arm is extended to all 68 targets, recorded in a row before that run, with its own budget check. No warm reruns; C is not run. (d) Contrasts, with the statistics of section 5 and the target-clustered bootstrap (10,000 replicates, seed 2026). Primary pairs HW(V5,V6) and HW(V6,G), each with HW minus RC of its first platform (RC(V5), RC(V6)) in each group; the reading against the second platform's RC is reported next to it. RC(V5) and RC(V6) are computed on the subset's targets of each group, so HW minus RC is the difference between HW over all the group's targets and RC over its subset targets, each resampled within its own targets. RC(G) uses all targets, paired. Headline, for each primary pair: the large minus control difference in HW minus RC for the top-1 flip rate, with a 95% interval from resampling targets within each group independently. Secondary: the success flip rate, flips that matter, HW(V5,G) and SD on each platform. Reading rule, per pair: if the interval lies above 0, the hardware effect grows with input size; if below 0, it shrinks; otherwise "no size difference detected at this sample size", stated with the interval's width. From the main study's per-target spread, the expected half-width is about 6.9 points for HW(V5,V6) and 8.2 for HW(V6,G). Variant A: the same statistics on the variant A targets (17 large, 11 control), a sensitivity analysis reported next to the primary result and not read with the rule. Exploratory, no reading rule: the per-seed top-1 flip of each primary pair against token count over the 68 targets, by logistic regression (slope per 256 tokens, 95% interval from the target-clustered bootstrap), with and without method as a covariate. M1: the reference is the rule-2 instance of the deposited model. Every M1 result (success flip, flips that matter, success rates) is flagged for EM (44 of 68 targets) and also reported on the X-ray subset (12 large, 12 control). The headline uses no reference structure. All targets are post-cutoff, so there is no post-cutoff subset. (e) Order: after follow-ups 11 and 12, under the parallel-arms row: on TPU, V5 then V6, after V6h5; on GPU, G after Gh5. Every run finishes by 2026-11-14 (results freeze 2026-11-15), else the arm is reported as incomplete. Launch by 2026-11-11 for the TPU arms (65.0 VM h in sequence) and by 2026-11-13 for G (21.6 h on 8 L4). (f) Cost, from `cloud/prices.csv` list prices (`drafts/fu10_cost.py`; process walls: main-study medians up to 1,024 tokens, the memory-limit runs of 2026-10-14 for 1,280 and 1,536; parts up to 8 h on TPU and 4 h on GPU, one target's runs in one part), expected and worst case: V5 on demand, 5 parts, 37.1 VM h, 57.88 and 79.56 USD; V6 Flex-start, 4 parts, 27.9 VM h, 37.63 and 50.98; G Spot, 39 parts, 165.3 VM h, 88.96 and 125.86. Total 184.47 expected, 256.40 worst. Cap 221 USD (1.2 times expected); floor 25 USD, after subtracting the expected cost of every running arm. The contingent r2 extension would add 32.66 (V5) and 21.02 (V6) and needs a cap amendment. (g) Stop rules: the inputs are frozen, the implementation is committed and the OSF addendum is registered before the first run; no run is dropped or selected on results; a preempted part is relaunched unchanged; each launch passes the budget rule; every run finishes by 2026-11-14. | Hardware flip rates above 1,024 tokens, on enough independent proteins |

(a) Targets of 11: `targets/build_repl_subset.py` runs the main builder's screen unchanged. The screen loop of `targets/build_posebusters_subset.py` moved into a function, `screen_v2`, without any other change; rerun into a scratch folder, the main builder writes `posebusters_subset.csv` and `posebusters_v2_screen.csv` byte for byte and `sources.json` identical except `built_utc`. With no arguments it finds the 60 sequences cached and queries nothing. `inputs/build_inputs.py` gains `--subset`, `--manifest` and `--input_dir`, with defaults `targets/posebusters_subset.csv`, `inputs/manifest.csv` and `data/inputs`. It also gains `--exclusions`, off by default. With the defaults the 60 inputs, rebuilt into a scratch folder, are byte-identical to `data/inputs/` and the manifest rows identical except `featurise_seconds`. Follow-up 11 runs `--subset targets/repl/repl_subset.csv --manifest inputs/repl/manifest.csv --input_dir data/inputs_repl --exclusions inputs/repl/excluded.csv`. Its MSAs go to the shared cache `data/msa/` (72 new sequences, none shared with the 60), with the server etiquette of `build_msas.py` unchanged. A target whose input AlphaFold3 rejects, or whose token count differs from the screen's, is left out of the manifest and listed in `inputs/repl/excluded.csv` with its cause, then recorded in a section 10 row before any run (the row's "excluded, with its cause"). A missing MSA stops the build, as before. Build of 2026-11-04: the 72 MSAs were queried from 07:30 to 16:27Z (`msa_query_utc` in the manifest), server `https://api.colabfold.com`, mode `env`. During the day the ColabFold server failed repeatedly on its side (time-outs, HTTP 400 "too many open files", downloads cut short with `IncompleteRead`); `build_msas.py` waited and retried under its unchanged backoff. Two fixes before the build completed, neither changing any query, MSA or input: `build_msas.py` also catches `http.client.HTTPException` as a network error (an `IncompleteRead` had stopped the script instead of waiting; SHA-256 `3d4e879f...`), and `build_inputs.py` creates the folder of the exclusions file before writing it (the first build stopped with `FileNotFoundError` on `inputs/repl/excluded.csv` before writing the manifest; rerun after the fix; SHA-256 `6bb6b7f6...`). From the afternoon the queries ran with `COLABFOLD_CONTACT` set, which adds a contact to the user agent as the server asks (`inputs/http_util.py`, unchanged; 31 of the 72 MSA records carry it). Result (`inputs/repl/manifest.json`, built 16:49:56Z, repository at `becdb3a` plus this branch): 72 of 72 inputs validated by AlphaFold3 (`af3_featurised`), token counts equal to the screen's, none excluded (`inputs/repl/excluded.csv` header only, SHA-256 `7035e7bc...`); `inputs/repl/manifest.csv` SHA-256 `eed43b649ce660a3d2ec77099a8e51d7cd0d5c5efe318960c7443789ccbe8eb5`; buckets 256: 5, 512: 67; seeds 1 to 5 in every input; inputs in `data/inputs_repl/`. (c) How the inputs reach the VM: as for follow-ups 1 and 6, the plan key `manifest: inputs/repl/manifest.csv`. `cloud/af3_run.sh` uploads that manifest and each target's `input_path` (`data/inputs_repl/<pdb_id>.json`), and `cloud/vm_af3_setup.sh` checks every input's SHA-256 against it with `harness/verify_inputs.py` before any run. No change to `cloud/`. (d) M1 of 11: `analysis/score_ligand.py` looks a target up in `targets/posebusters_subset.csv` first, then in `targets/repl/repl_subset.csv`, so the 60 are scored as before. Each of the 72 crystal structures, scored as a prediction of itself (`self_test`, an engineering check), is a success: 72 of 72. (e) Plans: `harness/plans/make_fu11_12_plans.py`, sibling of `make_fu7_10_plans.py`, using its sizing, deadline, lifetime and price functions. Each target's runs share one part (r1 and r2 of follow-up 11 on one VM, as RC requires). Parts are packed first-fit decreasing up to 8 h of job on TPU and 4 h on GPU, with deadline ceil(job min x 1.25 + 20) and timings from the source arm's main-study sessions. `--check` is up to date. Arms: V5r, V6r and Gr (`repl`; `tpu_xla` on v5e, `tpu_xla` on v6e, `l4_xla` on L4) on the 72 targets, seeds 1 to 5, r1 and r2, no warm reruns, plan key `manifest`; V6h5 and Gh5 (`prec5`; `tpu_xla_highest` on v6e, `l4_xla_highest` on L4) on the 60 targets, seeds 3 to 5, r1. Seeds 3 to 5 are in the frozen inputs' `modelSeeds` (1 to 5), so no `seeds_beyond_input`. Points the rows leave open, read literally: in follow-up 11, "HW minus RC" in the reading rule is HW minus RC of the pair's first platform (section 5's reference), with the reading against the second platform's RC reported next to it. "Every M1 result" covers the success flip rate, flips that matter and the success rate of the per-seed top-1. The main study's overall rates come from its own outputs (`primary_v2`); its bucket-512 rates are computed from its seed pairs on its 19 targets of that bucket. In follow-up 12, section 11's "shrinks to the RC level" is read as the main study's report reads it: HWh(V6,G) minus RC(V6) with an interval above 0 does not shrink (attributed to the platform); an interval containing 0 or below it does (attributed to the default precision). The two-seed results are copied from the main study and checked equal to those recomputed from the same sessions. It rebuilds the screen's state from its cache and the outcome of every checked entry (`pdb_screen_entries_amended.csv`), replays the selection and the control draw, and names nothing unless the replay gives exactly the 68 targets. Read literally: a large target's replacement is the next member of its 30% cluster that meets rules 1 to 4 with 1,025 to 1,536 tokens, in the order of rule 5; with none, the target is dropped, not replaced. A control target's replacement continues its stratum's draw where it stopped (the next clusters of the permutation, skipping clusters already taken), each cluster giving its first member of the stratum that meets rules 1 to 4 with at most 1,024 tokens; a member the screen never checked is checked with the screen's own function (its mmCIF from RCSB into the screen's cache). Tried on 2026-11-05 on four targets, as a test only: 8EXL to 8EXO, 9OMM to 7FRQ, 7KFO to 8C0S, 9DRA to 8K9V (5 mmCIF files added to the screen's cache; no file of `targets/` or `inputs/` written). No change to `cloud/`. (d) M1 (row item d, "the reference is the rule-2 instance of the deposited model"): `analysis/score_ligand.py` looks a target up in `targets/posebusters_subset.csv`, then `targets/repl/repl_subset.csv`, then `targets/large/large_subset.csv`; for the 60 and the 72 nothing changes (the defaults of every function, and the paths, are the same; `analysis/tests` pass). For a follow-up 10 target the crystal structure is the cached deposited mmCIF (`source_file`) and the reference ligand is `data/large_refs/<PDB>_ligand.sdf`, written by `build_large_subset.py`: the `ligand_asym` instance of model 1, heavy atoms, the first alternate location of the instance (as gemmi's `remove_alternative_conformations` keeps it, which `score_ligand.py` applies to the crystal), with bond orders and formal charges from AlphaFold3's own CCD entry (`alphafold3.data.tools.rdkit_utils.mol_from_ccd_cif`) and CCD atom names; CCD atoms the deposited instance lacks are left out (9FTR: C1 and C2 of A1IGB, 18 of 20 atoms). Pocket, superposition, symmetry-corrected RMSD and PoseBusters checks unchanged. There is no PoseBusters crystal protein file for these targets, so `--pb_protein crystal` is refused for them (the analysis uses the predicted protein, as the main study). Self-test (each deposited model scored as a prediction of itself, an engineering check; for these targets PoseBusters gets the crystal's own pocket chain, and the ligand's chain is named, since an oligomer can hold copies of the ligand with the same residue number): 62 of 68 successes, every one with RMSD 0.0. The 6 others also have RMSD 0.0 and fail only PoseBusters' `minimum_distance_to_protein` check (9DRA also `volume_overlap_with_protein`), a property of the deposited model: 8Y85 (EM, 4KU 1.44 Å from Lys842 NZ, a bond RCSB does not annotate as covalent), 9VU1 (EM, A1ETS O38 1.38 Å from Leu609 CD2), 9DRA (X-ray; the ligand is modelled only as alternate location B, occupancy 0.45, against protein conformation A; Tyr200 OH 2.0 Å), 8SI6, 8XB3 and 7UG0 (EM, short polar contacts of 2.1 to 2.3 Å). Their M1 success needs a PoseBusters-valid prediction against a reference that is itself not PoseBusters-valid; they stay in the analysis as registered, and the report lists them. Checks before any comparison: exit codes, one machine type per condition, every target in the manifest, none among the 60 or the 72, r2 on V5 and V6 covering the subset. First result: the bit identity of TPU recompilation on the subset (r2 against r1, sample by sample, mmCIF coordinates identical as written, as interpretation 8 of the plan reads "bit-identical"); if any target is not bit-identical, the report says so at its top, because the row's extension rule (r2 on that arm for all 68 targets, its own row and budget check) then applies. Contrasts in each group with section 5's statistics and the target-clustered bootstrap (10,000 replicates, seed 2026): HW(V5,V6), HW(V6,G), HW(V5,G) (secondary), RC(V5), RC(V6), RC(G), SD on each platform (r1). HW minus RC in each group: RC(V5) and RC(V6) on the group's subset targets, HW on all its targets, each resampled within its own targets; RC(G) on all targets, paired (the same resampled targets); when an arm's r2 covers every target (the extension), its RC is paired too, as the data say. Headline: per primary pair, the large minus control difference in HW minus RC(P) for the top-1 flip rate, each group resampled independently; reading rule as written (above 0, "the hardware effect grows with input size"; below 0, "it shrinks"; else "no size difference detected at this sample size", with the interval's width), applied only to the headline on all targets; the difference against RC(Q), the success flip rate and flips that matter are reported next to it, not read. Variant A: every flip statistic on its targets (17 large, 11 control), not read. M1: every M1 result (success flip, flips that matter, success rate of the per-seed top-1) carries the number of EM targets behind it and is also given on the X-ray targets (12 large, 12 control). Exploratory: per primary pair, the r1 per-seed top-1 flip against tokens/256 over all targets, logistic regression by maximum likelihood (Newton-Raphson; numpy, the analysis environment has no statsmodels), with and without a method indicator (EM 1); slope interval from the target-clustered bootstrap with the same replicates and seed, a replicate whose fit does not converge (as under complete separation) left out and counted. Completeness: r1 on 68 targets per arm, r2 on 68 for G and on the 20 for V5 and V6 (on 68 if extended); an incomplete arm is marked, nothing imputed. Both TPU types may then run at once, one arm each. The chains (`~/Desktop/af3_drafts/run_chain_v2.sh`, implementation row, item i): V5l starts on v5e as soon as V5r is complete, without waiting for V6h5 (`run_chain_v2.sh v5e after:v5r v5l`); V6l starts on v6e after V6h5 (`run_chain_v2.sh v6e after:v6h5 v6l`); Gl starts on L4 after Gh5 (`run_chain_v2.sh l4 after:gh5 gl`). The follow-up 11 and 12 chains of `run_chain.sh` continue unchanged (TPU: V5r, V6r, then V6h5 after Gr; GPU: Gr, then Gh5 after V6r), so V5l on v5e can run beside V6r or V6h5 on v6e and beside Gh5 on L4. Every wrapper is `retry_arm_par.sh` with `ALLOW_OTHER_LAUNCHERS=1`; each wrapper's quota counter counts only its own launchers, and with one arm per platform no two wrappers ever count the same platform: v5e and v6e at one each (`MAX_V5E=1`, `MAX_V6E=1`, the defaults), L4 at `MAX_L4=8` (the GPUS_ALL_REGIONS quota of 8, one L4 arm at a time; no H100 arm runs). The TPU quotas are per TPU family (`TPUS_PER_TPU_FAMILY`), and v5e and v6e ran one VM each at once in the main study's TPU wrapper run (row of 2026-10-12). Checked for shared state between concurrent wrappers (scripts read on 2026-11-05: `cloud/af3_run_many.sh`, `cloud/af3_run.sh`, `retry_arm_par.sh`, `run_chain.sh`, `run_chain_v2.sh`), for each pair among a v5e, a v6e and an L4 wrapper: run folders `results/af3/.many/<UTC second>/` (distinct unless two wrappers start a round in the same second; the second then stops with "exists" and retries 20 min later, as in the row of 2026-11-04); part locks `results/af3/.many/locks/<platform>_<plan>` (platform and plan names distinct per arm); session names `<UTC second>_<platform>_<plan>` and VM names `af3-run-<platform>-<UTC second>` (distinct across platforms); temporary files (`mktemp`); each wrapper reads its own summary from its own output (`retry_arm_par.sh`); quota counters per wrapper and platform (above). The chain ledger is shared by every chain and only appended to, one line per event; each chain reads the status of the arms it waits for and the worst case of every arm recorded complete. Nothing else is shared. The budget rule stays per arm (`credit_check_fu10.sh` for follow-up 10, with the expected cost of every running arm subtracted, follow-ups 11 and 12 included). | 2026-11-10 | Campaign simulation (label `campaign`), a new analysis of frozen outputs, specified after every result of the main study and of follow-ups 1 to 12 was read and before any computation; reported as a follow-up analysis, outside the pre-registered study, descriptive, whatever it shows. No run and no cloud resource. Question: when a scheduler mixes device types within one campaign, what fraction of the campaign's reported top-ranked samples changes, and by how many points does its benchmark success rate change, against the same campaign on one device type? Unit, two variants: (a) a job is one (target, seed), as this study ran it; its reported result is the seed's top-ranked sample (M2) and its M1 success; (b) a job is one target with every seed the pair shares, as AlphaFold3 runs several seeds in one process; its reported result is the top-ranked sample across those seeds (AlphaFold3's main output, full-precision `ranking_score`) and its M1 success. Policies (home device P, other device Q, source of the per-pair values, all r1): (1) L4 and A100 mixed: P = A, Q = G, `results/analysis/a100_20261018/` (HW(G,A), 60 targets, seeds 1 to 5); (2) A100 and H100 mixed: P = A, Q = H, `results/analysis/h100_20261104/` (HW(H,A), 60 targets, seeds 1 to 5); (3) CPU fallback: P = G, Q = C, `results/analysis/cpu60_20261018/` (HW(G,C), 60 targets, seeds 1 and 2); (4) cloud TPU and GPU: P = V6, Q = G, the main study `results/analysis/main_20261014/primary_v2/` (HW(V6,G), 60 targets, seeds 1 to 5) and follow-up 11 `results/analysis/repl_20261106/` (72 targets, seeds 1 to 5); (5) cloud TPU generations: P = V5, Q = V6, the same two folders (HW(V5,V6)); for policies 4 and 5 the main study and follow-up 11 are reported separately, not pooled. A job's seeds are those of the pair's rows; rates are over jobs; for unit (b), ties in the score go to the lowest seed, then sample. Shares: a fraction s of the campaign's jobs runs on Q, s = 0.1, 0.25, 0.5 and 1. The shares are assumptions, not measurements: the center guides (`paper/research/center_guides.csv`, 28 guides read on 2026-11-09) say whether a job script may land on another GPU type or fall back to the CPU, not how often it does, and no center publishes its job mix. Simulation, per policy, share and unit: each bootstrap replicate resamples targets with replacement, assigns each job of the resampled campaign to Q with probability s, independently, and compares the mixed campaign with the same campaign run entirely on P. Statistics: (i) changed, the fraction of jobs whose reported sample differs from the all-P campaign (the top-1 flip of the pair for a moved job, none for a job that stays on P); (ii) success changed, the fraction of jobs whose reported success differs; (iii) success points, the success rate of the mixed campaign minus that of the all-P campaign, in percentage points. Reference, beside each policy: the same campaign rerun entirely on P in a second, separately compiled process (RC(P) on the same unit and seeds: RC(A) for 1 and 2, RC(G) on seeds 1 and 2 for 3, RC(V6) and RC(V5), which are 0, for 4 and 5, with RC(G) printed beside policy 4), so that a mixed campaign is read against what a rerun on the pinned device already changes; C ran r1 only, so the CPU has no rerun reference. Point estimate: the expectation over assignments on the observed targets (s times the pair's rate for statistics i and ii; s times the pair's success difference for iii); 95% percentile interval over 10,000 replicates, seed 2026 (`numpy.random.default_rng(2026)`, one stream for the targets and the assignments, in a fixed order of policies, units and shares). Inputs: unit (a) the `pairs_seed.csv` rows of the named HW and RC contrasts; unit (b) `samples.csv` of the same folders (`target`, `seed`, `sample`, `platform`, `repetition`, `ranking_score`, `m1_success`), the main study's for G and V6 where a follow-up compares with them. A unit that could not be scored counts as not a success, as in section 4. Reading: descriptive, no reading rule, and no decision depends on it. Outputs: `results/analysis/campaign_<DATE>/` (`campaign.csv`, `references.csv`, `report.md`, `inputs.tsv` with the path and SHA-256 of every input, `run.log`), written by `analysis/campaign.py`, run by `DATE=<DATE> bash scripts/analysis_runs/campaign/reproduce.sh`; synthetic tests `analysis/tests/test_campaign.py`, run in the same command before the analysis. The code and tests were written before this row and are committed with it or before the run. | A campaign-level reading of the frozen per-pair results, asked for by the ISC reviews (`paper/review/review_isc_pc.md`) |
| 2026-11-10 | Ranking rules and their cost (label `ranking`), a new analysis of frozen outputs, specified after every result of the main study and of follow-ups 1 to 12 was read and before any computation; reported as a follow-up analysis, outside the pre-registered study, descriptive, whatever it shows. No run and no cloud resource. Question: can a rule for choosing the reported prediction reduce its dependence on the platform, and at what cost in seeds, that is in AlphaFold3 processes and time per structure? Data, per sample: the full-precision `ranking_score`, `ranking_score_summary` (the two-decimal value AlphaFold3 writes in its summary file) and `m1_success`, from `samples.csv` of the main study `results/analysis/main_20261014/primary_v2/` (V5, V6, G; r1 and r2; seeds 1 to 5), follow-up 8 `results/analysis/seeds_20261102/` (V5 and G, r1, seeds 6 to 20, joined with the main study's V5 and G r1 for seeds 1 to 5), follow-up 9 `results/analysis/h100_20261104/` (H, r1 and r2, seeds 1 to 5) and follow-up 3 `results/analysis/a100_20261018/` (A, r1 and r2, seeds 1 to 5; the A side of HW(H,A), as `analysis/h100.py` reads it). Pairs: HW(V5,G) on seeds 1 to 20 for rule (i); HW(V5,V6), HW(V6,G) and HW(H,A) on seeds 1 to 5 for rules (ii) and (iii); references RC(G), RC(A) and RC(H) on seeds 1 to 5 (RC(V5) and RC(V6) are 0; Gx has no second run, so rule (i) has no recompilation reference above n = 5). Rules: (i) seed consensus: the reported prediction is the sample with the highest full-precision ranking score among the 5n samples of n seeds, AlphaFold3's own rule for its main output, for n = 1, 2, 5, 10 and 20; the 20 seeds are split into 20/n disjoint blocks of consecutive seeds (1 to n, n + 1 to 2n, ...), each (target, block) one unit, so every n uses all 20 seeds; RC(G) at n = 1 and n = 5 from the main study's seeds 1 to 5. (ii) Tie tolerance: on each platform the reported set is the samples whose score is at least the top score minus t; a tie is declared when the set holds two or more samples, that is when the top-two margin is at most t; t = 0, 0.0002, 0.0005, 0.001, 0.002, 0.005 and 0.01 (0.01 is the step of the two-decimal score); per seed (n = 1) and across seeds 1 to 5 (n = 5). (iii) Rounded score: ranking by `ranking_score_summary`, ties to the lowest (seed, sample) index, against ranking by the full-precision score; per seed and across seeds 1 to 5. (iv) Exploratory model, point 3 of the numerics review (`paper/review/review_numerics.md`): for each contrast of the exploratory analysis (c) (row of 2026-10-14), the predicted per-seed top-1 flip rate obtained by adding to each (target, seed)'s five first-condition scores the vector of the five score differences (second minus first condition, same sample index) of another (target, seed) of the same contrast, so that the five components stay together and keep the correlation of samples that share one trunk, averaged over every such vector from the other targets (every copy of the unit's own target left out; for blocks, the two-sample model uses the median block margin); against the observed rate and the two-sample model of (c); and, for HW(V5,G) on seeds 1 to 20, the same for the top-1 across the 5n samples of a block of n seeds (n = 1, 2, 5), with difference vectors of blocks of other targets. Statistics: as the campaign row: paired bootstrap over targets, 10,000 replicates, seed 2026, 95% percentile intervals; differences between rules or between n on the same resampled targets. Reading: descriptive, no reading rule; (iv) is exploratory.
